================================================================================
FILE: .github\workflows\deploy.yml
================================================================================

name: Deploy Astro to GitHub Pages

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  workflow_dispatch:

permissions:
  contents: read
  pages: write
  id-token: write

jobs:
  build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
      
      - name: Set up Node.js
        uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: 20
          cache: npm
          
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: npm ci
        
      - name: Build site
        run: npm run build
        
      - name: Upload artifact
        uses: actions/upload-pages-artifact@v3
        with:
          path: ./dist

  deploy:
    needs: build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    environment:
      name: github-pages
      url: ${{ steps.deployment.outputs.page_url }}
    steps:
      - name: Deploy to GitHub Pages
        id: deployment
        uses: actions/deploy-pages@v4

================================================================================
FILE: astro.config.mjs
================================================================================

import { defineConfig } from 'astro/config';
import tailwind from '@astrojs/tailwind';

// Fully static output -- builds to plain HTML/CSS/JS in dist/.
// No server runtime required to host it anywhere.
export default defineConfig({
  site: 'https://DadmehrRahbari.github.io',
  base: '/RTAI', // Required if deploying to a standard repository subpath
  output: 'static',
  integrations: [
    tailwind({
      applyBaseStyles: false, // we bring our own base styles in src/styles/global.css
    }),
  ],
});

================================================================================
FILE: README.md
================================================================================

# RTAI website

A static marketing site built with [Astro](https://astro.build) + [Tailwind CSS](https://tailwindcss.com).
No React, no CMS, no database, no server runtime required to host it -- `npm run build` produces plain
HTML/CSS/JS in `dist/`.

## Run it -- one step

**Windows:** double-click `start.bat`.
**macOS/Linux:** double-click `start.sh`, or run `./start.sh` in a terminal (`chmod +x start.sh` once first if needed).

Either one installs dependencies automatically on first run (only if `node_modules` doesn't exist yet),
starts the dev server, and opens your default browser to the site -- no other commands needed. The only
prerequisite is [Node.js](https://nodejs.org) (LTS) being installed; if it isn't, the script tells you and
stops instead of failing with a confusing error.

Prefer the terminal? `npm run launch` does exactly the same thing, cross-platform.

There is no `requirements.txt` here because that's a Python convention -- this is a Node.js/Astro project,
where `package.json` (dependency list) + `package-lock.json` (exact locked versions, already included)
is the equivalent. `npm install` reads those two files the same way `pip install -r requirements.txt`
reads a requirements file.

## Continuous integration

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `npm run build` automatically on every push and every pull request to
`main`. If a change breaks the build, GitHub shows a red X on the PR before anyone merges it -- this is
validation only, it doesn't deploy anything (Netlify/Vercel/Cloudflare Pages handle the actual deploy,
each on their own trigger). No setup needed on your end beyond having this file in the repo; GitHub picks
it up automatically.

## Is this harder to host than a classic static HTML project?

No -- there's exactly one extra step, and after that step it's identical.

An old-school HTML/CSS/JS project is upload-and-done: the files you edit are the files a browser reads.
This project adds one step before that point: `npm run build` turns the `src/` folder into that same kind
of plain `dist/` folder -- `.html`, `.css`, `.js`, nothing else, no server-side code, no database. Once
`dist/` exists, it is byte-for-byte as simple to host as an old static site:

- **Drag and drop, no git at all** -- run `npm run build` locally, then drag the `dist` folder onto
  [Netlify Drop](https://app.netlify.com/drop) or Cloudflare Pages' manual upload. Live in seconds, no
  account setup beyond signing in.
- **Classic shared hosting (cPanel, FTP)** -- run `npm run build`, then upload everything *inside* `dist/`
  into `public_html/` (or wherever your host's document root is) using FileZilla, cPanel's File Manager, or
  any FTP client -- exactly the same motion as uploading an old static site, because at that point it is one.
- **Any Linux server** -- see the `rsync` + nginx example earlier in this file.
- **Git-based (Netlify/Vercel/Cloudflare Pages)** -- the recommended path from earlier in this file; the
  host runs the build step for you on every push, so you never manually touch `dist/` at all.

The only genuine difference from a classic HTML project: you can't edit a file on the live server directly
and see it change (there's a build step between source and output). Everything downstream of that build is
exactly as simple as it's always been.



Open `src/site.config.ts`. Company name, tagline, nav links, footer links, email, location, and social
links all live there. Every page and component reads from it -- nothing is hardcoded per-page.

## Local development

Requires [Node.js](https://nodejs.org) 18 or newer.

```bash
npm install
npm run dev
```

Opens a live-reloading preview at `http://localhost:4321`.

## Build

```bash
npm run build
```

Outputs a fully static site to `dist/`. Preview the production build locally with `npm run preview`.

## Deploy

### Option A -- Git-based (recommended)

Push this repo to GitHub, then connect it to any of:

- **Vercel** -- import the repo, it auto-detects Astro, zero config.
- **Netlify** -- import the repo, build command `npm run build`, publish directory `dist`. Also enables
  the contact form on `/contact` with zero backend code (see below).
- **Cloudflare Pages** -- same build command/publish directory as above.

Every push to `main` rebuilds and redeploys automatically, with a preview URL per pull request.

### Option B -- Any plain Linux host, no Node.js required on the server

Node is only needed to *build* the site, not to *serve* it.

```bash
npm run build
rsync -avz dist/ user@yourserver:/var/www/RTAI/
```

Point nginx, Caddy, or Apache at that folder as a static site. Example minimal nginx block:

```nginx
server {
    listen 80;
    server_name example.com;
    root /var/www/RTAI;
    index index.html;
    error_page 404 /404.html;
}
```

### Option C -- Docker (portable, reproducible)

```dockerfile
FROM node:20-alpine AS build
WORKDIR /app
COPY . .
RUN npm install && npm run build

FROM nginx:alpine
COPY --from=build /app/dist /usr/share/nginx/html
```

## Contact form

`src/pages/contact.astro` ships with a form pre-wired for **Netlify Forms** (`data-netlify="true"`) --
if you deploy on Netlify, submissions arrive with zero backend code, spam-filtered by default. If you
deploy elsewhere, swap the `<form>`'s attributes for a service like Formspree, or point it at your own
API endpoint; nothing else on the page needs to change.

## Project structure

```
start.bat               Windows one-click launcher
start.sh                 macOS/Linux one-click launcher
scripts/
  launch.mjs             installs deps if needed, starts dev server, opens browser
src/
  site.config.ts       single source of truth for brand text, nav, contact info
  layouts/
    BaseLayout.astro   head, fonts, nav, footer wrapper
  components/
    Nav.astro
    Footer.astro
    StatusTicker.astro live telemetry-style hero animation (respects prefers-reduced-motion)
  styles/
    global.css         Tailwind layers + design-token utility classes (.panel, .btn-primary, etc.)
  pages/
    index.astro
    product.astro
    industries.astro
    technology.astro
    case-studies.astro
    company.astro
    contact.astro
    careers.astro
public/
  favicon.svg
  robots.txt
```

## Design tokens

Defined in `tailwind.config.mjs`: an `ink` (graphite-navy) background scale, `paper` text scale, and
three accent colors -- `amber` (attention/signature), `teal` (verified/healthy), `fault` (error states,
used sparingly). Fonts: Space Grotesk (display), Inter (body), JetBrains Mono (data/status/labels),
loaded from Google Fonts in `BaseLayout.astro`.


================================================================================
FILE: scripts\launch.mjs
================================================================================

#!/usr/bin/env node
// One script, zero manual steps: installs dependencies if they're missing,
// starts the dev server, and opens your default browser automatically
// (via Astro's own --open flag). Works the same on Windows, macOS, and
// Linux -- called by start.bat, start.sh, or `npm run launch` directly.

import { spawn, spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

const root = path.dirname(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)));
process.chdir(root);

const npmCmd = process.platform === 'win32' ? 'npm.cmd' : 'npm';

if (!existsSync(path.join(root, 'node_modules'))) {
  console.log('[launch] First run detected -- installing dependencies (npm install)...');
  const install = spawnSync(npmCmd, ['install'], { stdio: 'inherit', shell: true });
  if (install.status !== 0) {
    console.error('[launch] npm install failed -- see the error above. Fix that, then run this again.');
    process.exit(install.status ?? 1);
  }
}

console.log('[launch] Starting the dev server and opening your browser...');
console.log('[launch] Press Ctrl+C in this window to stop it.\n');

const dev = spawn(npmCmd, ['run', 'dev', '--', '--open'], { stdio: 'inherit', shell: true });
dev.on('exit', (code) => process.exit(code ?? 0));

<script>
  (function () {
    var reduce = window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;
    if (reduce) return;

    var b = document.getElementById('pulse-b');
    var arrow = document.getElementById('pulse-arrow');
    if (!b || !arrow) return;

    var phase = 0;
    function apply(state) {
      b.classList.remove('is-fault', 'is-healing', 'is-healthy');
      if (state === 'fault') {
        b.classList.add('is-fault');
        arrow.className = 'ti ti-alert-triangle text-sm text-fault-400';
      } else if (state === 'healing') {
        b.classList.add('is-healing');
        arrow.className = 'ti ti-refresh text-sm text-amber-400';
      } else {
        b.classList.add('is-healthy');
        arrow.className = 'ti ti-arrows-vertical text-sm text-teal-400';
      }
    }

    setInterval(function () {
      phase = (phase + 1) % 3;
      apply(phase === 0 ? 'healthy' : phase === 1 ? 'fault' : 'healing');
    }, 1600);
  })();
</script>

<script>
  const toggle = document.getElementById('nav-toggle');
  const mobile = document.getElementById('nav-mobile');
  const iconOpen = document.getElementById('nav-icon-open');
  const iconClose = document.getElementById('nav-icon-close');

  toggle?.addEventListener('click', () => {
    const isOpen = mobile?.classList.toggle('hidden') === false;
    toggle.setAttribute('aria-expanded', String(isOpen));
    iconOpen?.classList.toggle('hidden', isOpen);
    iconClose?.classList.toggle('hidden', !isOpen);
  });
</script>

FILE: src\components\StatusTicker.astro

<div
  id="status-ticker"
  class="panel font-mono text-xs leading-relaxed overflow-hidden"
  role="status"
  aria-live="polite"
>
  <div class="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-x-6 gap-y-2">
    <span class="text-paper-500">FRAME <span id="st-frame" class="text-paper-100">004821</span></span>
    <span class="text-paper-500">TIER0 <span id="st-tier0" class="status-chip ok">ok</span></span>
    <span class="text-paper-500">META <span id="st-meta" class="status-chip ok">ok</span></span>
    <span class="text-paper-500">TIER1 <span id="st-tier1" class="status-chip ok">ok</span></span>
    <span class="text-paper-500">TRUST <span id="st-trust" class="text-teal-400">0.98</span></span>
  </div>
  <div class="mt-3 text-paper-300">
    <span class="text-amber-400">&gt;</span> <span id="st-log">watching every frame, verifying its own weights against a server-issued fingerprint</span>
  </div>
</div>

<script>
  const reduceMotion = window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;

  const el = {
    frame: document.getElementById('st-frame'),
    tier0: document.getElementById('st-tier0'),
    meta: document.getElementById('st-meta'),
    tier1: document.getElementById('st-tier1'),
    trust: document.getElementById('st-trust'),
    log: document.getElementById('st-log'),
  };

  function setChip(node, state) {
    if (!node) return;
    node.classList.remove('ok', 'warn', 'fault');
    node.classList.add(state);
    node.textContent = state === 'ok' ? 'ok' : state === 'warn' ? 'degraded' : 'fault';
  }

  if (reduceMotion || !el.frame) {
    // Respect reduced-motion: leave the static "healthy" state in place, no animation.
  } else {
    let frame = 4821;
    let phase = 0; // 0 = healthy, 1 = fault detected, 2 = resyncing, 3 = recovered
    const messages = [
      'watching every frame, verifying its own weights against a server-issued fingerprint',
      'hash mismatch on tier-0 integrity check, quarantining node, requesting federated resync',
      'fetching a fresh, server-verified model, adopting new integrity fingerprint',
      'resync verified. trust restored, sustained-drift gate reset',
    ];

    setInterval(() => {
      frame += Math.floor(Math.random() * 30) + 5;
      if (el.frame) el.frame.textContent = String(frame).padStart(6, '0');

      if (phase === 0 && Math.random() < 0.12) phase = 1;
      else if (phase === 1) phase = 2;
      else if (phase === 2) phase = 3;
      else if (phase === 3) phase = 0;

      if (phase === 0) {
        setChip(el.tier0, 'ok');
        setChip(el.meta, 'ok');
        setChip(el.tier1, 'ok');
        if (el.trust) { el.trust.textContent = '0.98'; el.trust.className = 'text-teal-400'; }
      } else if (phase === 1) {
        setChip(el.tier0, 'fault');
        if (el.trust) { el.trust.textContent = '0.00'; el.trust.className = 'text-fault-400'; }
      } else if (phase === 2) {
        setChip(el.tier0, 'warn');
        if (el.trust) { el.trust.textContent = '0.00'; el.trust.className = 'text-amber-400'; }
      } else if (phase === 3) {
        setChip(el.tier0, 'ok');
        if (el.trust) { el.trust.textContent = '0.97'; el.trust.className = 'text-teal-400'; }
      }
      if (el.log) el.log.textContent = messages[phase];
    }, 1800);
  }
</script>

import '../styles/global.css';
import Nav from '../components/Nav.astro';
import Footer from '../components/Footer.astro';
import { site } from '../site.config';

interface Props {
  title: string;
  description?: string;
}

const { title, description = site.description } = Astro.props;
const pageTitle = title === site.name ? site.name : `${title} · ${site.name}`;
---

<!doctype html>
<html lang="en" class="scroll-smooth">
  <head>
    <meta charset="UTF-8" />
    <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1.0, viewport-fit=cover" />
    <!-- Matches the ink-950 background: keeps the Android/Chrome address bar and
         iOS Safari status bar dark instead of defaulting to white on a dark page. -->
    <meta name="theme-color" content="#0B0F14" />
    <meta name="apple-mobile-web-app-status-bar-style" content="black-translucent" />
    <meta name="apple-mobile-web-app-capable" content="yes" />
    <!-- iOS auto-detects and blue-links anything number-shaped (phone numbers,
         dates) by default, off, since we style our own links deliberately. -->
    <meta name="format-detection" content="telephone=no" />
    <link rel="icon" type="image/svg+xml" href="/favicon.svg" />
    <title>{pageTitle}</title>
    <meta name="description" content={description} />

    <meta property="og:type" content="website" />
    <meta property="og:title" content={pageTitle} />
    <meta property="og:description" content={description} />
    <meta property="og:url" content={site.url} />
    <meta name="twitter:card" content="summary_large_image" />

    <link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com" />

---
import BaseLayout from '../layouts/BaseLayout.astro';
import { site } from '../site.config';
---

<BaseLayout title="Company" description="Why we're building an independent trust layer for autonomous systems.">
  <section class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 pt-20 pb-16 sm:pt-28 sm:pb-20 border-b border-ink-600">
    <p class="eyebrow">Company</p>
    <h1 class="mt-5 max-w-3xl font-display text-4xl sm:text-5xl font-semibold leading-tight">
      Autonomy shouldn't have to choose between capable and accountable.
    </h1>
  </section>

  <section class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28">
    <div class="grid gap-10 md:grid-cols-[0.9fr_1.1fr] md:gap-16">
      <p class="eyebrow">Why we exist</p>
      <div class="space-y-6 text-paper-300">
        <p>
          Perception models keep getting more capable and more opaque at the same time. Most teams
          shipping autonomous systems today have no independent answer to a simple question: how do you
          know, in real time, that the model making decisions right now is the same trustworthy model you
          validated before deployment?
        </p>
        <p>
          We started with a single, narrow belief: a monitor that only reasons about a model's outputs is
          not enough if the monitor itself can silently break. So we built the self-healing layer first,
          not as an afterthought bolted onto a dashboard, but as the thing the whole system is organized
          around.
        </p>
        <p class="text-paper-100">
          {site.name} exists to make that answer available to any team building autonomy that has to work,
          and fail safely, without someone watching every frame.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>

  <section class="border-y border-ink-600 bg-ink-900/40">
    <div class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28">
      <p class="eyebrow">How we work</p>
      <div class="mt-14 grid gap-6 sm:grid-cols-3">
        <div class="panel">
          <h3 class="font-display text-lg font-semibold">Measure, don't assert</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">Every claim we make about detection or recovery is backed by a benchmark we can show you.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="panel">
          <h3 class="font-display text-lg font-semibold">Black-box by default</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">We design for teams who can't or won't hand over their model's weights, and build our own trust around that constraint, not against it.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="panel">
          <h3 class="font-display text-lg font-semibold">Escalate, don't hide</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">A system that keeps quietly retrying a fault it can't fix is worse than one that asks for help. We design for the second.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>

  <section class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28">
    <p class="eyebrow">Where we are</p>
    <h2 class="mt-4 font-display text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-semibold">{site.location}</h2>
    <p class="mt-4 max-w-xl text-paper-300">
      Building close to Estonia's deep-tech and autonomous-systems community, with a reference deployment
      already running end-to-end.
    </p>
  </section>

  <section class="border-t border-ink-600">
    <div class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28 text-center">
      <h2 class="font-display text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-semibold max-w-xl mx-auto">
        Building autonomous systems and want an independent second opinion?
      </h2>
      <div class="mt-8 flex justify-center gap-4">
        <a href={`${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}/contact" class="btn-primary">Talk to us</a>
        <a href={`${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}/careers" class="btn-secondary">See open roles</a>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>
</BaseLayout>

import BaseLayout from '../layouts/BaseLayout.astro';
import IntegrationHub from '../components/IntegrationHub.astro';
import { site } from '../site.config';

const pillars = [
  {
    n: '01',
    title: 'Detect',
    body: 'Every frame is checked against multiple independent signals, embedding-space novelty, sensor health, temporal consistency, spatial plausibility, so no single trick fools the system.',
  },
  {
    n: '02',
    title: 'Diagnose',
    body: 'A fault-localization layer decides where the problem actually is: the perception model, the monitor watching it, or a physical sensor, before anything is repaired.',
  },
  {
    n: '03',
    title: 'Heal',
    body: 'Hardware faults trigger a verified resync from a trusted source. Genuine drift triggers an in-process fine-tune, validated against held-out data before it is ever trusted.',
  },
  {
    n: '04',
    title: 'Learn',
    body: 'What one unit learns strengthens the fleet through reliability-weighted aggregation, low-trust updates are down-weighted automatically, not blindly averaged in.',
  },
];

const stats = [
  { value: '154x', label: 'lighter than the perception model it audits' },
  { value: '< 1', label: 'frame to detect a hardware-level fault' },
  { value: '0', label: 'raw weights or gradients required from your model' },
  { value: '4', label: 'independent failure modes covered, not one confidence score' },
];
---

      </h1>
      <p class="mt-6 max-w-2xl text-base sm:text-lg text-paper-300">
        {site.name} is a lightweight, independent monitor that watches your drone, robot, or vehicle's
        perception system frame by frame, catches silent failures a confidence score would miss, and
        recovers automatically, without ever needing access to the model it's watching.
      </p>
      <div class="mt-8 flex flex-wrap gap-4">
        <a href={`${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}/contact" class="btn-primary">Talk to us</a>
        <a href={`${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}/product" class="btn-secondary">See how it works</a>

        <IntegrationHub />
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>

  <!-- Problem -->
  <section class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28">
    <div class="grid gap-10 md:grid-cols-[0.9fr_1.1fr] md:gap-16">
      <div>
        <p class="eyebrow">The problem</p>
        <h2 class="mt-4 font-display text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-semibold leading-tight">
          Perception models fail silently. Confidence scores don't help.
        </h2>
      </div>
      <div class="space-y-6 text-paper-300">
        <p>
          A network can be simultaneously wrong and highly confident, a documented failure mode across
          nearly every deep learning deployment. If a model's own softmax output is the only signal
          available, whatever fools its classification head also fools any check built purely from that
          same head.
        </p>
        <p>
          A monitor built entirely from a model's outputs can also fail on its own: bit-flips, memory
          corruption, and compute-path faults affect the watcher just as much as the watched. A system
          that never checks its own integrity has no way to tell "the world genuinely changed" from
          "my own reasoning is broken."
        </p>
        <p class="text-paper-100">
          {site.name} is built around both halves of that problem: independent, multi-signal detection,
          and a monitor that audits its own trustworthiness before it ever audits yours.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>

  <!-- Pillars -->

FILE: src\pages\industries.astro

---
import BaseLayout from '../layouts/BaseLayout.astro';

const industries = [
  {
    title: 'Drones & UAVs',
    summary: 'Perception integrity with a real safety gate in the flight loop, not a log line after the fact.',
    points: [
      'Reliability score gates the flight controller directly, a confirmed fault can hold position before a bad frame is ever acted on.',
      'Runs alongside real flight telemetry (GPS quality, battery, IMU) as additional independent evidence.',
      'Local self-healing works without a persistent uplink, a disconnected unit keeps detecting, buffering, and recovering, and syncs when it reconnects.',
    ],
  },
  {
    title: 'Defense & ISR',
    summary: 'Black-box by design: your perception model\'s weights, gradients, and internals never have to leave your control.',
    points: [
      'Decision-making reads only a model\'s outputs, embeddings, boxes, confidence, so it can watch systems you can\'t or won\'t expose.',
      'Weight-integrity hashing and rollback ladders give an auditable, tamper-evident trail for every model state a unit has run.',
      'Escalation-over-looping: a persistent fault raises a human alert instead of retrying indefinitely, which matters when the cost of a wrong autonomous action is high.',
    ],
  },
  {
    title: 'Industrial robotics',
    summary: 'Local self-healing keeps a line or a warehouse fleet moving without a round trip to the cloud.',
    points: [
      'Fine-tune, validate, and hot-reload happen in-process, on real buffered frames from the actual operating environment.',
      'A bounded, validated rollback ladder means a bad update can always be reverted to the last known-good state.',
      'Fleet-wide learning is optional, not required, a single unit is fully capable of detecting and healing on its own.',
    ],
  },
];
---

<BaseLayout title="Industries" description="Where a silent perception failure is not an acceptable outcome.">
  <section class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 pt-20 pb-16 sm:pt-28 sm:pb-20 border-b border-ink-600">
    <p class="eyebrow">Industries</p>
    <h1 class="mt-5 max-w-3xl font-display text-4xl sm:text-5xl font-semibold leading-tight">
      Autonomy that has to keep working, and fail safely, unsupervised.
    </h1>
    <p class="mt-6 max-w-2xl text-lg text-paper-300">
      The pattern is the same everywhere it matters most: a perception model operating far from a human
      operator, where a silent failure is more dangerous than a loud one.
    </p>
  </section>

  <section class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28 space-y-20">
    {
      industries.map((ind) => (
        <div class="grid gap-8 md:grid-cols-[0.8fr_1.2fr] md:gap-16">
          <div>
            <h2 class="font-display text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-semibold leading-tight">{ind.title}</h2>
            <p class="mt-4 text-paper-300">{ind.summary}</p>
          </div>
          <ul class="space-y-4">
            {ind.points.map((p) => (
              <li class="panel text-sm text-paper-300">{p}</li>
            ))}
          </ul>
        </div>
      ))
    }
  </section>

  <section class="border-t border-ink-600">
    <div class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28 text-center">
      <h2 class="font-display text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-semibold max-w-xl mx-auto">
        Don't see your exact use case? The architecture is deliberately domain-agnostic.
      </h2>
      <p class="mt-5 text-paper-300 max-w-xl mx-auto">
        Anything that emits an embedding, a confidence score, and a bounding box or mask can be monitored.
      </p>
      <div class="mt-8">
        <a href={`${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}/contact" class="btn-primary">Talk to us</a>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>
</BaseLayout>

import BaseLayout from '../layouts/BaseLayout.astro';
import { site } from '../site.config';

const tiers = [
  {
    tag: 'Tier 0',
    title: 'Hardware integrity',
    cadence: 'Every frame',
    body: 'A real cryptographic hash of the monitor\'s own weights, checked against a fingerprint issued from outside the device, never from its own past state. A mismatch quarantines the node and requests a verified resync. No local repair is attempted on a system that can no longer vouch for itself.',
  },
  {
    tag: 'Meta',
    title: 'Behavioral self-test',
    cadence: 'Every few seconds',
    body: 'Weight checks catch storage corruption but miss compute-path faults and logic bugs. Four independent checks, canary inputs, output-distribution sanity, jitter stability, and fleet-consistency, catch the failures a hash alone cannot.',
  },
  {
    tag: 'Tier 1',
    title: 'Perceptual drift',
    cadence: 'Every frame, sustained',
    body: 'A sequential change-detection test on out-of-distribution signal, so a single noisy frame never triggers a false alarm, only genuine, sustained drift does. A second check tells "the world changed" apart from "this is a genuinely new class," since only the former is safe to learn from automatically.',
  },
  {
    tag: 'Integrity',
    title: 'Perception model audit',
    cadence: 'Configurable',
    body: 'Where you control the deployed perception model, the same weight-hash and a repeatability canary close the gap between "the output looks anomalous" and "the model itself is compromised", so a corrupted model is never mistaken for a legitimate discovery.',
  },
];

const remedies = [
  {
    title: 'Verified resync',
    body: 'A hardware or behavioral fault in the monitor is never repaired locally. The only remedy is a fresh, server-verified model, a node can\'t certify its own health against itself.',
  },
  {
    title: 'Fine-tune, validate, roll back',
    body: 'Confirmed drift triggers a candidate fine-tune on real, buffered frames. It\'s adopted only if it doesn\'t regress against a held-out validation split, and every accepted version joins a bounded rollback ladder, nothing unvalidated can ever enter it.',
  },
  {
    title: 'Escalate, don\'t loop',
    body: 'A fault that keeps recurring after repeated automated fixes stops retrying and raises a human alert instead. Self-healing that never gives up is a liability, not a feature.',
  },
];

---
import BaseLayout from '../layouts/BaseLayout.astro';

const faults = [
  { name: 'Hardware fault, obvious', detail: 'weights pushed far out of range', catches: 'Hash check and legacy checksum both catch this, the easy case.' },
  { name: 'Hardware fault, subtle', detail: 'weights permuted, magnitude sum unchanged', catches: 'Only a real cryptographic hash catches this. A sum-of-magnitudes checksum is structurally blind to it.' },
  { name: 'Behavioral fault', detail: 'compute path broken, weights untouched', catches: 'Only the behavioral self-test suite catches this, a weight hash cannot, by construction.' },
  { name: 'Genuine domain shift', detail: 'sustained, real distribution change', catches: 'Confirmed only after sustained-drift detection, never on a single frame.' },
  { name: 'Transient noise', detail: 'one noisy frame, not sustained', catches: 'Measured as a false-trigger rate, the system should not react to this at all.' },
];

const references = [
  'Rahbari, D., Daneshtalab, M., & Jenihhin, M. (2026). Aux-AI: A Federated Independent Oversight Framework for Trust-Aware Industrial Autonomy.',
];

<BaseLayout title="Technology & research" description="Methodology, benchmark design, and results behind the self-healing architecture.">
  <section class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 pt-20 pb-16 sm:pt-28 sm:pb-20 border-b border-ink-600">
    <p class="eyebrow">Technology &amp; research</p>
    <h1 class="mt-5 max-w-3xl font-display text-4xl sm:text-5xl font-semibold leading-tight">
      We measure our own failure modes before claiming we've solved them.
    </h1>
    <p class="mt-6 max-w-2xl text-lg text-paper-300">
      Every claim on this page is backed by a controlled fault-injection benchmark, run against real
      perception checkpoints where possible, engineered specifically to demonstrate the blind spots of
      simpler approaches rather than just assert them.
    </p>
  </section>

  <section class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28">
    <p class="eyebrow">Benchmark design</p>
    <h2 class="mt-4 max-w-2xl font-display text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-semibold leading-tight">
      Five fault types, three arms, measured the same way every time.
    </h2>
    <p class="mt-6 max-w-2xl text-paper-300">
      Every trial compares three arms: no self-healing at all, a reconstructed baseline representative of
      a simpler local-checksum approach, and the full tiered architecture, so the value of each layer is
      visible, not assumed.
    </p>

    <div class="mt-14 overflow-x-auto">
      <table class="w-full text-left text-sm border-collapse">
        <thead>
          <tr class="border-b border-ink-600 font-mono text-xs uppercase tracking-wider text-paper-500">
            <th class="py-3 pr-4">Fault type</th>
            <th class="py-3 pr-4">What it simulates</th>
            <th class="py-3">What it proves</th>
          </tr>
        </thead>
        <tbody>
          {
            faults.map((f) => (
              <tr class="border-b border-ink-700 align-top">
                <td class="py-4 pr-4 font-semibold text-paper-100 whitespace-nowrap">{f.name}</td>
                <td class="py-4 pr-4 text-paper-500 font-mono text-xs">{f.detail}</td>
                <td class="py-4 text-paper-300">{f.catches}</td>
              </tr>
            ))
          }
        </tbody>
      </table>
    </div>
  </section>

  <section class="border-y border-ink-600 bg-ink-900/40">
    <div class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28">
      <p class="eyebrow">What we measure</p>
      <h2 class="mt-4 max-w-2xl font-display text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-semibold leading-tight">
        Detection rate, false positives, localization accuracy, and how long recovery takes.
      </h2>
      <div class="mt-14 grid gap-6 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4">
        <div class="panel">
          <h3 class="font-display text-base font-semibold">Detection rate</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">Per fault type, across repeated trials at a fixed, known injection point.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="panel">
          <h3 class="font-display text-base font-semibold">False-positive rate</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">Measured on clean runs and on transient noise specifically, lower is strictly better.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="panel">
          <h3 class="font-display text-base font-semibold">Localization accuracy</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">Does the diagnosis match the actual injected fault, only meaningful for an architecture that localizes at all.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="panel">
          <h3 class="font-display text-base font-semibold">Recovery latency</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">Frames from fault onset to a verified, trusted state, and segmentation quality before/after a heal cycle.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>

  <section class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28">
    <p class="eyebrow">Design principles</p>
    <div class="mt-8 grid gap-8 md:grid-cols-2">
      <div>
        <h3 class="font-display text-lg font-semibold">No fabricated numbers</h3>
        <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">
          If a metric can't be honestly measured for a given scenario, for instance, accuracy metrics on
          a live flight with no ground-truth anomaly label, it's reported as unavailable, not estimated
          or faked to fill a chart.
        </p>
      </div>
      <div>
        <h3 class="font-display text-lg font-semibold">Real checkpoints, real substitutes clearly labeled</h3>
        <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">
          Benchmarks prefer real, trained models automatically when available, and fall back to small,
          explicitly-labeled substitutes only when they aren't, so results are never silently generated
          from a toy model.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>

    <div class="mt-16 border-t border-ink-600 pt-10">
      <p class="eyebrow">References</p>
      <ul class="mt-4 space-y-2 text-sm text-paper-500 font-mono">
        {references.map((r) => <li>{r}</li>)}
      </ul>
    </div>
  </section>

  <section class="border-y border-ink-600 bg-ink-900/40">
    <div class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28">
      <p class="eyebrow">Where the monitor actually lives</p>
      <h2 class="mt-4 max-w-2xl font-display text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-semibold leading-tight">
        Not one placement. Three, each doing a job the others structurally can't.
      </h2>
      <p class="mt-6 max-w-2xl text-paper-300">
        This isn't a compromise, it's the pattern that both the MLOps-monitoring literature and the
        safety-critical robotics literature converge on independently. A single placement is never enough:
        something has to be fast enough to gate an action, something has to see across the whole fleet, and
        something has to produce the evidence a safety or procurement review will actually ask for.
      </p>

      <div class="mt-14 grid gap-6 sm:grid-cols-3">
        <div class="panel">
          <span class="status-chip ok">Inline</span>
          <h3 class="mt-4 font-display text-base font-semibold">Every frame, next to Big-AI</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">
            Tier 0, Meta, and Tier 1 run in-process, fast enough to gate the actuator before a bad frame is
            ever acted on. This is the same principle formalized in runtime-assurance research for
            autonomous vehicles, pairing a fast, unverifiable perception layer with a monitor that can
            intervene in real time, an idea academic work calls the Simplex and Perception Simplex
            architectures. The honest difference: those architectures typically wrap a provably-bounded
            classical detector; ours is a learned, statistical trust layer, traded for the ability to watch
            any perception model rather than one with a hand-derived safety proof. Closing that gap for the
            single highest-stakes failure mode is on our roadmap, not a solved problem.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div class="panel">
          <span class="status-chip ok">Fleet-wide</span>
          <h3 class="mt-4 font-display text-base font-semibold">A dedicated, federated service</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">
            The federated coordinator and fleet-stats endpoint run asynchronously, aggregating trust across
            every connected unit, too slow and too expensive to run per frame, but this is where a
            quarantined node's fresh, verified reference actually comes from. Standard MLOps monitoring
            architecture converges on the same split: a lightweight in-process check paired with a
            dedicated service for anything that needs a wider view.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div class="panel">
          <span class="status-chip ok">Batch / CI</span>
          <h3 class="mt-4 font-display text-base font-semibold">A gate before release, not after</h3>
          <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-paper-300">
            The fault-injection benchmark on this page runs as a release gate, not a one-off report --
            producing the detection-rate, false-positive, and recovery-latency numbers a safety review or
            an AI governance framework will ask for before anything ships. Too slow for every frame, exactly
            right for every release.
          </p>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>

  <section class="border-t border-ink-600">
    <div class="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6 py-20 sm:py-28 text-center">
      <h2 class="font-display text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-semibold max-w-xl mx-auto">
        Want the benchmark run against your own perception model?
      </h2>
      <div class="mt-8">
        <a href={`${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}/contact" class="btn-primary">Talk to us</a>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>
</BaseLayout>

@tailwind base;
@tailwind components;
@tailwind utilities;

:root {
  /* ============================================================
     GLOBAL UI SCALE, the one parameter to change site-wide text
     (and most spacing) size, now and in the future.

     Every Tailwind text-* size (text-xs, text-sm, text-base, text-lg,
     text-2xl, ...) and most spacing utilities (p-*, gap-*, etc.) are
     defined in rem, which is relative to THIS root font-size. Raise
     or lower this one value and the entire site scales together --
     no need to touch text-xs -> text-sm across dozens of files.

     100%   = 16px base (browser default, feels small for a marketing site)
     112.5% = 18px base (current default below, noticeably more readable)
     125%   = 20px base (large-print / accessibility-first)

     On mobile, we bump it slightly further via the media query below,
     since phone screens are held closer and benefit from a touch
     larger base than desktop.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------- */
  --ui-scale: 112.5%;
}

@layer base {
  html {
    font-size: var(--ui-scale);
    scroll-behavior: smooth;
  }
  @media (max-width: 640px) {
    html {
      font-size: calc(var(--ui-scale) * 1.05);
    }
  }
  body {
    @apply bg-ink-950 text-paper-100 font-body antialiased;
  }
  h1, h2, h3, h4 {
    @apply font-display text-paper-100;
  }
  ::selection {
    @apply bg-amber-500/30 text-paper-100;
  }
  a:focus-visible,
  button:focus-visible,
  input:focus-visible,
  textarea:focus-visible {
    outline: 2px solid theme('colors.amber.500');
    outline-offset: 2px;
  }
  @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
    *, *::before, *::after {
      animation-duration: 0.001ms !important;
      animation-iteration-count: 1 !important;
      transition-duration: 0.001ms !important;
      scroll-behavior: auto !important;
    }
  }
}

@layer components {
  /* Blueprint-style panel: hairline border + corner ticks, used instead of
     rounded "cards" so the surface reads as schematic/engineering, not
     generic SaaS. */
  .panel {
    @apply relative border border-ink-600 bg-ink-800/60 p-6 sm:p-8;
  }
  .panel::before,
  .panel::after {
    content: '';
    @apply absolute w-2.5 h-2.5 border-amber-500/70 pointer-events-none;
  }
  .panel::before {
    @apply top-0 left-0 border-t border-l;
  }
  .panel::after {
    @apply bottom-0 right-0 border-b border-r;
  }

  .eyebrow {
    @apply font-mono text-xs tracking-[0.25em] uppercase text-teal-400;
  }

  .btn-primary {
    @apply inline-flex items-center gap-2 bg-amber-500 text-ink-950 font-body font-semibold
           px-5 py-3 text-sm hover:bg-amber-400 transition-colors;
  }
  .btn-secondary {
    @apply inline-flex items-center gap-2 border border-ink-600 text-paper-100 font-body font-semibold
           px-5 py-3 text-sm hover:border-paper-500 transition-colors;
  }

  .status-chip {
    @apply inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 font-mono text-xs tracking-wide uppercase
           border px-2 py-1;
  }
  .status-chip.ok {
    @apply border-teal-500/40 text-teal-400 bg-teal-500/5;
  }
  .status-chip.warn {
    @apply border-amber-500/40 text-amber-400 bg-amber-500/5;
  }
  .status-chip.fault {
    @apply border-fault-500/40 text-fault-400 bg-fault-500/5;
  }

  .grid-bg {
    background-image: theme('backgroundImage.grid-lines');
    background-size: theme('backgroundSize.grid');
  }

  .pulse-target {
    transition: background-color 0.5s, border-color 0.5s;
  }
  .pulse-target.is-fault {
    @apply border-fault-500;
    background-color: rgb(226 84 61 / 0.14);
  }
  .pulse-target.is-healing {
    @apply border-amber-500;
    background-color: rgb(242 169 59 / 0.14);
  }
  .pulse-target.is-healthy {
    @apply border-teal-500;
    background-color: rgb(47 184 166 / 0.1);
  }
}

FILE: start.bat

@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0"

where node >nul 2>nul
if errorlevel 1 (
  echo Node.js was not found on this machine.
  echo Install it from https://nodejs.org ^(LTS version^), then double-click this file again.
  pause
  exit /b 1
)

node scripts\launch.mjs

echo.
echo Server stopped.
pause


================================================================================
FILE: start.sh
================================================================================

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -e
cd "$(dirname "$0")"

if ! command -v node >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "Node.js was not found on this machine."
  echo "Install it from https://nodejs.org (LTS version), then run this again."
  exit 1
fi

node scripts/launch.mjs
